[codex] ## Project Overview
[codex] 
[codex] Session Gateway provides session-based edge authorization for browser-based clients in the Budget Analyzer application.
[codex] 
[codex] **Purpose**: Protects sensitive tokens from browser exposure by managing OAuth2 authorization-code flows server-side and issuing HTTP-only session cookies. Writes session data as Redis hashes that the ext_authz HTTP service reads directly for per-request authorization at the Istio ingress. Sessions slide forward through a frontend heartbeat that operates against local Redis state. Owns the OAuth2 and session lifecycle endpoints, while the frontend owns bare `/login`.
[codex] 
[codex] **Key Responsibilities**:
[codex] - Manages OAuth2/OIDC authorization-code flows with Auth0 for browser clients
[codex] - Fetches user roles and permissions from the permission-service on login
[codex] - Writes session data (userId, roles, permissions, expiry) as Redis hashes (`session:{id}`)
[codex] - Maintains per-user session indexes in Redis (`user_sessions:{userId}`) for targeted revocation
[codex] - The ext_authz HTTP service reads these same hashes for ingress authorization — no separate schema
[codex] - Issues HTTP-only, Secure, SameSite session cookies to browsers
[codex] - Provides session heartbeat (`GET /auth/v1/session`) — extends session TTL based on local Redis state
[codex] - Owns `/oauth2/**`, `/auth/**`, `/login/oauth2/**`, and `/logout`
[codex] - Exposes internal session revocation for permission-service (`DELETE /internal/v1/sessions/users/{userId}`)
[codex]

[codex] ### Architectural Simplicity (KISS)
[codex] 
[codex] **Primary rule: Keep it simple.** Choose the simplest implementation that correctly handles realistic inputs, states, and failure modes. Simplicity must not come at the expense of security, data integrity, or required behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] - Put validation in the layer that owns the rule: request models and controllers validate request shape and syntax; services validate business invariants, ownership, persistence state, and cross-entity rules.
[codex] - Do not duplicate API validation in the service layer when every call reaches the service through the validated API contract. Add service-level validation when another caller can bypass that contract or when the service owns the rule.
[codex] - Do not add a guard, fallback, or custom exception path for a state made impossible by an enforced boundary or invariant.
[codex] - At external or asynchronous boundaries, handle plausible failures explicitly because they are outside the local code's control.
[codex] - Before adding a defensive branch, identify how the state can arise and what the caller or system can usefully do in response. If neither is concrete, omit the branch.
[codex] - Prefer a direct implementation and established project patterns over speculative abstractions or extension points.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Architecture Principles
[codex] 
[codex] - **Defense-in-Depth Security**: Session-based edge authorization in the hybrid architecture (Istio ingress → Session Gateway or NGINX, with ext_authz enforcing `/api/*`)
[codex] - **ext_authz-Based Authorization**: The Istio ingress Envoy proxy calls the ext_authz HTTP service, which reads session hashes directly from Redis and injects X-User-Id/X-Roles/X-Permissions headers into API requests
[codex] - **Token Protection**: IDP tokens are consumed at login time only; nothing from Auth0 is persisted after the session is created. Browser only sees an opaque session cookie
[codex] - **Browser-Only Auth**: Session Gateway exclusively serves browser clients. There is no token exchange or bearer token surface
[codex] - **Behind Istio Ingress**: Browser auth and OAuth2 protocol endpoints reach Session Gateway through Istio ingress; bare `/login` is frontend-owned and served through NGINX
[codex] - **Stateful Sessions**: Redis hashes (`session:{id}`) provide distributed session storage — identity, roles, permissions, expiry — read by both Session Gateway and the ext_authz service
[codex] - **Targeted Session Revocation**: Redis sets (`user_sessions:{userId}`) index all active sessions for a user so permission-service can revoke them without scanning `session:*`
[codex] - **Sliding Sessions**: Frontend heartbeat (`GET /auth/v1/session`) resets session TTL based on local Redis state. Session Gateway extends unconditionally on every heartbeat call; the frontend is responsible for calling only when the user is active (idle users = no heartbeat = session expires naturally). Auth0 is not contacted during heartbeat
[codex] 
[codex] ## Service Architecture
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Session-Based Edge Authorization + OAuth2 Client
[codex] 
[codex] **Architecture Flow**:
[codex] ```text
[codex] Browser → Istio Ingress (:443)
[codex]   ├─ /oauth2/*, /auth/*, /login/oauth2/*, /logout
[codex]   │      → Session Gateway (:8081) ← OAuth2 → Auth0
[codex]   │           ├─ Permission Service (:8086) [email/displayName]
[codex]   │           └─ Redis (:6379) [session:*]
[codex]   ├─ /login, /* → NGINX (:8080) → budget-analyzer-web
[codex]   └─ /api/* → ext_authz HTTP service (:9002) → NGINX (:8080) → Backend Services
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Find all Java source files
[codex] find src/main/java -name "*.java" | sort
[codex] 
[codex] # Find configuration classes
[codex] grep -r "class.*Config" src/main/java
[codex] 
[codex] # Check OAuth2 configuration
[codex] grep -r "oauth2" src/main/resources/
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Port Summary**:
[codex] - **443**: Istio Ingress Gateway (browser entry point, SSL termination, ext_authz integration)
[codex] - **8080**: NGINX Gateway (frontend and API routing)
[codex] - **8081**: Session Gateway (behind Istio ingress, receives auth/session lifecycle requests)
[codex] - **9002**: ext_authz HTTP service (called by the ingress Envoy proxy on `/api/*`)
[codex] - **8090**: ext_authz health endpoint
[codex] - **8086**: Permission Service (user roles and permissions)
[codex] - **6379**: Redis (session storage)
[codex] 
[codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] 
[codex] **Principle**: Spring WebFlux reactive stack with OAuth2 and custom Redis session management.
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View all dependencies

[codex] 
[codex] **Stack Components**:
[codex] - **Web**: Spring WebFlux (reactive)
[codex] - **Security**: Spring Security OAuth2 Client
[codex] - **Sessions**: Custom Redis hash sessions via `SessionWriter`/`SessionReader` (not Spring Session)
[codex] - **Service Common (service-web)**: Reactive HTTP logging, correlation IDs, safe logging, exception handling
[codex] - **Permission Client**: WebClient for reactive HTTP calls to permission-service
[codex] - **Build**: Gradle with Kotlin DSL
[codex] - **Cache**: Redis (AOF persistence)
[codex] - **Identity Provider**: Auth0 (OAuth2/OIDC)
[codex] 
[codex] **Service-Common Integration**:
[codex] - Auto-configured reactive utilities from `org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>`
[codex] - Provides: HTTP request/response logging, distributed tracing correlation IDs, safe logging with sensitive data masking, global exception handling
[codex] - OAuth2ResourceServerSecurityConfig explicitly excluded (Session Gateway is OAuth2 Client, not Resource Server)
[codex] - DataSource/JPA auto-configuration excluded (no database required)

[codex] 
[codex] **Component Types**:
[codex] 
[codex] **Configuration** (src/main/java/.../config/):
[codex] - SecurityConfig: OAuth2 login, authorization, entry points, return URL handling, permission fetching on login success, session creation
[codex] - SessionConfig: Clock bean
[codex] - OAuth2ClientConfig: OAuth2 client, authorization request repository, and authorized client repository configuration
[codex] - WebClientConfig: WebClient bean for external HTTP calls
[codex] 
[codex] **Controllers** (src/main/java/.../api/):
[codex] - UserController: Returns current authenticated user info
[codex] - LogoutController: Session deletion, cookie clearing, and Auth0 logout
[codex] - SessionController: Session heartbeat — validates the local Redis session and extends its TTL
[codex] 
[codex] **Security** (src/main/java/.../security/):
[codex] - RedirectUrlValidator: Validates redirect URLs to prevent open redirect attacks
[codex] - RedisAuthorizationRequestRepository: Stores OAuth2 authorization requests in Redis (keyed by state parameter), carries return URL through the OAuth2 round-trip
[codex] - RedisSessionSecurityContextRepository: Resolves session cookie → Redis hash → SecurityContext for authenticated requests
[codex] - ExchangeServerOAuth2AuthorizedClientRepository: Stores/loads OAuth2 authorized clients per-exchange (not in WebSession)
[codex] - SessionPrincipal: Authentication principal holding session data
[codex] 
[codex] **Services** (src/main/java/.../service/):
[codex] - PermissionServiceClient: Reactive WebClient that fetches user roles and permissions from the permission-service; passes email/displayName as query params (no bearer auth — platform network isolation and mesh policy enforcement)
[codex] 
[codex] **Session** (src/main/java/.../session/):
[codex] - SessionWriter: Creates, updates, and deletes session Redis hashes (`session:{id}`)
[codex] - SessionReader: Reads and deserializes session hashes from Redis
[codex] - SessionData: Record holding deserialized session fields
[codex] - SessionHashFields: Constants for Redis hash field names (user_id, roles, permissions, expires_at, etc.)
[codex] - SessionCookieHelper: Manages session cookies (set, clear, read)
[codex] 
[codex] **Service-Common Utilities** (auto-configured from service-web):
[codex] - ReactiveHttpLoggingFilter: HTTP request/response logging (replaces RequestLoggingWebFilter)
[codex] - ReactiveCorrelationIdFilter: Adds correlation IDs for distributed tracing
[codex] - ReactiveApiExceptionHandler: Global exception handling for WebFlux
[codex] - SafeLogger (org.budgetanalyzer.core.logging): Opt-in safe logging utilities — `SafeLogger.toJson()` for `@Sensitive` masking, `SafeLogger.mask()` for string masking, `SafeLogger.truncateId()` for truncating identifiers (session IDs, OAuth2 state)

[codex] 
[codex] **Authentication Flow Endpoints**:
[codex] - `GET /oauth2/authorization/idp` - Initiates OAuth2 login (auto-configured)
[codex]   - Optional `?returnUrl=` parameter: Specifies where to redirect after successful authentication
[codex]   - Example: `/oauth2/authorization/idp?returnUrl=/dashboard`
[codex]   - Security: All returnUrl values validated by RedirectUrlValidator (same-origin only)
[codex] - `GET /login/oauth2/code/idp` - OAuth2 callback (receives code from Auth0)
[codex] - `GET /logout` - Invalidates session, clears cookies, redirects to Auth0 logout
[codex] - Bare `/login` is not a Session Gateway endpoint. It is a frontend route that initiates `GET /oauth2/authorization/idp`.
[codex] 
[codex] **Return URL Flow**:
[codex] After successful authentication, the redirect priority is:
[codex] 1. **Explicit returnUrl parameter**: If `?returnUrl=/path` was provided to `/oauth2/authorization/idp` (carried through OAuth2 round-trip via `RedisAuthorizationRequestRepository`)
[codex] 2. **Default**: Redirects to `/` if no returnUrl was provided
[codex] 
[codex] If the OAuth2 callback fails after the flow started with `?returnUrl=...`, Session Gateway
[codex] redirects to `/login?error=auth_failed&returnUrl=...` so the frontend can retry the flow without
[codex] losing the original deep link.
[codex] 
[codex] **Return URL Security**:
[codex] - All redirect URLs validated by `RedirectUrlValidator` (src/main/java/.../security/RedirectUrlValidator.java:27)
[codex] - Only same-origin URLs allowed (prevents open redirect attacks)
[codex] - Rejects: external URLs, protocol-relative URLs, `javascript:`, `data:`, and other malicious schemes
[codex] - Invalid URLs safely default to `/` redirect
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Heartbeat Endpoint**:
[codex] - `GET /auth/v1/session` - Session heartbeat: validates the local Redis session and extends its TTL
[codex]   - Reads session from Redis via cookie
[codex]   - If the cookie points at a missing or expired Redis session: clears the stale cookie and returns 401
[codex]   - If session is healthy: resets `expires_at` + Redis key TTL (sliding window)
[codex]   - Response: `{ "userId": "...", "roles": [...], "expiresAt": &lt;epoch> }`
[codex]   - Auth0 is **not** contacted during heartbeat — validity depends entirely on local Redis state
[codex]   - 401 if no valid session
[codex] 
[codex] **Heartbeat Responsibility Contract**:
[codex] Session Gateway extends the session unconditionally on every `GET /auth/v1/session` call — it does not track or evaluate user activity. The responsibility split is:
[codex] - **Frontend owns the activity decision**: It tracks user activity (mouse movement, keyboard input, tab focus, etc.) and only calls the heartbeat while the user is active
[codex] - **Session Gateway owns the extension**: Every heartbeat call resets `expires_at` and the Redis key TTL to a fresh `session.ttl-seconds` window
[codex] - **Idle timeout is frontend-driven**: When the frontend detects the user is idle, it stops calling the heartbeat. The session TTL (default 15 min) lapses naturally and Redis expires the key — the user is logged out on their next interaction
[codex] - **No server-side idle tracking**: Session Gateway has no concept of "idle." An open browser tab that keeps calling the heartbeat on a fixed timer without checking activity would keep the session alive indefinitely — this is a frontend bug, not a feature
[codex] 
[codex] **User Endpoints**:
[codex] - `GET /auth/v1/user` - Returns current authenticated user information including roles and the resolved permission list. This endpoint is the frontend's source of truth for action-level permission gating — roles are for layout (`AdminRoute`), permissions are for actions. Response shape: `UserInfoResponse` carries `permissions: string[]` alongside `roles: string[]`.
[codex] - `DELETE /internal/v1/sessions/users/{userId}` - Internal service-to-service revocation of all indexed sessions for a user
[codex]   - Returns 204 whether sessions existed or not
[codex]   - Intended for permission-service during user deactivation
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: Session Gateway does not proxy downstream routes. It only handles auth/session lifecycle endpoints. The Istio ingress routes `/api/*` through ext_authz → NGINX, and `/*` through NGINX directly.
[codex]

[codex] 
[codex] **Configuration Categories**:
[codex] 
[codex] **OAuth2 Client** (Auth0):
[codex] - Client ID, secret, issuer URI
[codex] - Scopes: openid, profile, email
[codex] - Redirect URI for callback
[codex] - Authorization-code flow only — no refresh tokens are requested
[codex] - Recommended Auth0 dashboard values and rationale are documented in [docs/auth0-settings.md](docs/auth0-settings.md)
[codex] 
[codex] **Session**:
[codex] - `session.key-prefix` (`SESSION_KEY_PREFIX`): Redis key prefix for session hashes (default: `session:`)
[codex] - `session.ttl-seconds` (`SESSION_TTL_SECONDS`): TTL for session keys in seconds (default: `900`)
[codex] - `session.oauth2-state-ttl-seconds` (`SESSION_OAUTH2_STATE_TTL_SECONDS`): TTL for OAuth2 authorization request state in Redis (default: `900` / 15 min). Must be long enough for MFA enrollment, SSO handoffs, or slow IDP interactions
[codex] - `session.cookie.name` (`SESSION_COOKIE_NAME`): Public browser session cookie name (default: `BA_SESSION`)
[codex] - `session.cookie.domain-override` (`SESSION_COOKIE_DOMAIN_OVERRIDE`): Optional parent-domain override. Default is unset, which emits host-only cookies
[codex] - `session.cookie.secure` (`SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE`): HTTPS-only cookies (default: `true`)
[codex] - `session.cookie.same-site` (`SESSION_COOKIE_SAME_SITE`): SameSite attribute (default: `Strict`; accepts `Strict`, `Lax`, or `None`, case-insensitively)
[codex] 
[codex] **Permission Service**:
[codex] - `permission-service.base-url` (`PERMISSION_SERVICE_URL`): Base URL for the permission-service (default: `http://permission-service:8086`)
[codex] - Endpoint called: `GET /internal/v1/users/{idpSub}/permissions?email={email}&displayName={displayName}`
[codex] - No bearer auth — relies on platform network isolation and mesh policy enforcement
[codex] 
[codex] **Redis Connection**:
[codex] - Redis connection (host, port, username, password)
[codex] - TLS configuration via Spring SSL bundles
[codex] 
[codex] **Logging**:
[codex] - OAuth2 client debug logging
[codex] - Session management logging
[codex] 
[codex] **HTTP Logging** (from service-common):
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.enabled`: Enable/disable HTTP logging
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.log-level`: Log level (DEBUG, INFO, etc.)
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-request-body`: Log request bodies
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-response-body`: Log response bodies
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-request-headers`: Log request headers
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-response-headers`: Log response headers
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-query-params`: Log query parameters
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.include-client-ip`: Log client IP addresses
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.max-body-size`: Maximum body size to log (bytes)
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.exclude-patterns`: List of path patterns to exclude (e.g., `/actuator/**`)
[codex] - `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.log-errors-only`: Only log requests that result in errors
[codex] 
[codex] Example configuration (application.yml lines 97-113):
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] budgetanalyzer:
[codex]   service:
[codex]     http-logging:
[codex]       enabled: true
[codex]       log-level: DEBUG
[codex]       include-request-body: true
[codex]       include-response-body: true
[codex]       max-body-size: 10000
[codex]       exclude-patterns:
[codex]         - /actuator/**
[codex]         - /login/oauth2/code/**
[codex] ```

[codex] ### Troubleshooting
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick Commands**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] REDIS_OPS_USERNAME=$(kubectl get secret redis-bootstrap-credentials -n infrastructure -o jsonpath='{.data.ops-username}' | base64 -d)
[codex] REDIS_OPS_PASSWORD=$(kubectl get secret redis-bootstrap-credentials -n infrastructure -o jsonpath='{.data.ops-password}' | base64 -d)
[codex] 
[codex] # Check if Redis is accessible
[codex] kubectl exec -n infrastructure pod/redis-0 -- redis-cli --tls --cacert /tls-ca/ca.crt --user "$REDIS_OPS_USERNAME" --pass "$REDIS_OPS_PASSWORD" --no-auth-warning PING
[codex] 
[codex] # View session data in Redis
[codex] kubectl exec -n infrastructure pod/redis-0 -- redis-cli --tls --cacert /tls-ca/ca.crt --user "$REDIS_OPS_USERNAME" --pass "$REDIS_OPS_PASSWORD" --no-auth-warning KEYS "session:*"
[codex] 
[codex] # Check application health
[codex] curl http://localhost:8081/actuator/health
[codex] 
[codex] # View application logs
[codex] kubectl logs deployment/session-gateway
[codex] 
[codex] # Test OAuth2 flow
[codex] curl -v http://localhost:8081/oauth2/authorization/idp
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Common Issues**:
[codex] 
[codex] **Redirect Loop at Auth0**:
[codex] - Check Auth0 callback URL matches configuration
[codex] - Verify session cookie is being set (check browser dev tools)
[codex] - Review OAuth2 client logs for error details
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Not Persisting**:
[codex] - Verify Redis connection in application.yml
[codex] - Check Redis StatefulSet pod is running: `kubectl get pod -n infrastructure redis-0`
[codex] - Inspect Redis session keys with the `redis-ops` credentials shown above
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Not Created After Login**:
[codex] - Check that permission-service is running and reachable at `permission-service.base-url`
[codex] - Verify session hash exists in Redis: `redis-cli HGETALL "session:{session-id}"`
[codex] - Verify `session.key-prefix` matches the ext_authz service's expected prefix
[codex] - Enable debug logging: `logging.level.org.budgetanalyzer.sessiongateway=DEBUG`
[codex] 
[codex] **Heartbeat Returns 401**:
[codex] - Verify session cookie is present and the corresponding session hash exists in Redis
[codex] - The Redis key may have expired naturally (TTL lapsed) — the user must log in again
[codex] - The session may have been revoked through `DELETE /internal/v1/sessions/users/{userId}`
[codex] 
[codex] **502 Bad Gateway**:
[codex] - Ensure the Istio ingress and NGINX gateway are healthy in the shared platform
[codex] - Verify network connectivity: `curl https://api.budgetanalyzer.localhost/health`
[codex] 
[codex] ## Integration Points
[codex] 
[codex] **Upstream (Receives From)**:
[codex] - Istio ingress gateway routing `/oauth2/**`, `/auth/**`, `/login/oauth2/**`, and `/logout`
[codex] - Browser login page `/login`, which is frontend-owned and initiates `/oauth2/authorization/idp`
[codex] 
[codex] **Downstream (Sends To)**:
[codex] - **Auth0**: OAuth2 authorization-code flow at login, logout return URL
[codex] - **Permission Service** (port 8086): Fetches user roles and permissions on login (no bearer auth — platform network isolation)
[codex] - **Redis**: Session storage as hashes (`session:{id}`) — read by both Session Gateway and the ext_authz HTTP service
[codex] 
[codex] **Data Flow**:
[codex] 1. Browser loads `/login` through Istio ingress → NGINX → frontend
[codex] 2. Frontend initiates `GET /oauth2/authorization/idp`
[codex] 3. Istio ingress routes the OAuth2 request to Session Gateway
[codex] 4. Session Gateway completes OAuth2 with Auth0 and fetches permissions from permission-service
[codex] 5. Session Gateway writes a session hash to Redis (`session:{id}`) and sets the session cookie
[codex] 6. Frontend periodically calls `GET /auth/v1/session` (heartbeat) — extends session TTL based on local Redis state
[codex] 7. Browser later calls `/api/*`
[codex] 8. Istio ingress Envoy calls the ext_authz HTTP service, which reads the session hash from Redis and injects X-User-Id/X-Roles/X-Permissions
[codex] 9. NGINX forwards the validated API request to the backend service
[codex] 10. Backend services read claims from request headers
[codex] 
[codex] **Architecture Documentation**:
[codex] For detailed architecture diagrams and security design:
[codex] - [../orchestration/docs/architecture/authentication-implementation-plan.md](../orchestration/docs/architecture/authentication-implementation-plan.md)
[codex] - [../orchestration/docs/architecture/security-architecture.md](../orchestration/docs/architecture/security-architecture.md)
[codex] 
[codex] ## Security Considerations
[codex] 
[codex] **Token Protection**:
[codex] - IDP tokens are consumed at login time only and never written to Redis
[codex] - Browser only sees an opaque session cookie; all sensitive data lives in Redis
[codex] - Session hash deleted on logout
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Hash Security**:
[codex] - Sessions stored as Redis hashes under `session:{id}` with configurable TTL (default 15 min)
[codex] - Fields: `user_id`, `idp_sub`, `email`, `display_name`, `picture`, `roles` (comma-joined), `permissions` (comma-joined), `created_at`, `expires_at` (unix timestamps)
[codex] - The ext_authz HTTP service reads these same hashes directly from Redis — no cryptographic verification needed (Redis is trusted internal infrastructure)
[codex] - Session IDs are opaque UUIDs — no sensitive data encoded in the cookie value itself
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Cookies**:
[codex] - HttpOnly: Not accessible via JavaScript
[codex] - Secure: HTTPS only in production
[codex] - SameSite: CSRF protection
[codex] - Public cookie contract: `BA_SESSION` by default; any framework `SESSION` cookie is internal implementation detail, not the browser auth contract
[codex] - Host-only by default: no `Domain` attribute unless `session.cookie.domain-override` is set
[codex] - Domain override is a workaround/escape hatch, not the primary path
[codex] - Short TTL: 15 minutes default
[codex] 
[codex] **OAuth2 Best Practices**:
[codex] - PKCE enabled for authorization code flow
[codex] - State parameter for CSRF protection
[codex] - Redirect URI validation
[codex] 
[codex] **Session Lifecycle**:
[codex] - Frontend heartbeat (`GET /auth/v1/session`) keeps the session alive against local Redis state — Auth0 is not contacted after login
[codex] - Targeted revocation is available through `DELETE /internal/v1/sessions/users/{userId}` (called by permission-service)
[codex] - Operational defaults: 15-minute session TTL, 2-minute frontend heartbeat cadence
[codex] - Recommended Auth0 dashboard values that pair with these defaults are documented in [docs/auth0-settings.md](docs/auth0-settings.md)
[codex] 
[codex] **No CORS Needed**:
[codex] Same-origin architecture eliminates CORS complexity. Browser traffic stays on the same origin (`app.budgetanalyzer.localhost`), with `/login` served by the frontend and auth protocol endpoints handled by Session Gateway behind the same ingress.
[codex]

[codex] ## Related Services
[codex] 
[codex] Session Gateway is part of the Budget Analyzer microservices ecosystem:
[codex] 
[codex] **Direct Dependencies**:
[codex] - **NGINX Gateway**: Frontend/API gateway and downstream route target when requests reach Session Gateway directly
[codex] - **ext_authz HTTP Service**: Istio ingress authorization target — validates sessions from Redis and injects claims headers
[codex] - **Permission Service**: Provides user roles and permissions (called at login, no bearer auth)
[codex] - **Redis**: Session persistence (hashes under `session:{id}`)
[codex] - **Auth0**: Identity provider (login-time only)
[codex] 
[codex] **Indirect (via Istio ingress and NGINX)**:
[codex] - **Transaction Service**: Business logic for transactions
[codex] - **Currency Service**: Currency conversion
[codex] - **Budget Analyzer Web**: React frontend (served through NGINX)
[codex] 
[codex] **Repository Links**:
[codex] - Orchestration: https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration
[codex] - Session Gateway: https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/session-gateway
[codex] 
[codex] ## Best Practices
[codex] 
[codex] 1. **Secure Token Storage**: Never expose Auth0 tokens to the browser — IDP tokens are consumed at login time only, browser only sees an opaque session cookie
[codex] 2. **Comprehensive Logging**: Enable OAuth2 debug logging for troubleshooting
[codex] 3. **Session Timeout**: Balance security (short timeout) vs UX (longer timeout). Frontend heartbeat provides sliding window — session only dies if browser stops calling `GET /auth/v1/session`
[codex] 4. **Graceful Logout**: Delete session hash from Redis, clear cookie, and redirect to Auth0 logout
[codex] 5. **Health Checks**: Monitor Redis connectivity and Auth0 availability
[codex] 6. **Environment Parity**: Use same Auth0 tenant structure for dev/staging/prod
[codex] 7. **Return URL Validation**: All returnUrl redirects automatically validated by RedirectUrlValidator - no additional validation needed
[codex] 8. **Safe Logging**: SafeLogger is **opt-in** — use `SafeLogger.toJson(obj)` for objects with `@Sensitive` fields, `SafeLogger.mask(value)` for sensitive strings, `SafeLogger.truncateId(sessionId)` for session IDs and OAuth2 state values. Never log raw session IDs, OAuth2 code/state, or identity tokens.
[codex] 9. **HTTP Logging**: Configure `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.*` appropriately - disable or reduce verbosity in production. OAuth2 callback path (`/login/oauth2/code/**`) is excluded from HTTP logging (defense-in-depth).
[codex] 10. **Session Key Prefix Alignment**: `session.key-prefix` must match the ext_authz service's expected prefix so it can find session hashes
[codex] 11. **Permission-Service Dependency**: Login fails if permission-service is unreachable (permissions are required)
[codex] 12. **Heartbeat Interval**: Frontend should call `GET /auth/v1/session` every ~2 min **only while the user is active**. The current default is a 15-minute session TTL. Session Gateway extends unconditionally — if the frontend calls on a fixed timer without checking activity, sessions never expire for open tabs
[codex]

[codex] ../service-common
[codex] 
[codex] ### Goal
[codex] 
[codex] Discover platform/library updates and expose the actual BOM-managed dependency tree.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Scope
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate config, dependency-submission CI, and nearest repository documentation.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Non-goals
[codex] 
[codex] No BOM changes, overrides, library releases, build-logic redesign, or sibling edits.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Required context
[codex] 
[codex] Read this repository's `AGENTS.md`, version catalog, modules and build workflows,
[codex] plus orchestration's shared preset and service-common artifact-resolution guide.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Execution steps
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Add a thin Renovate config extending the shared preset. Verify extraction from
[codex]    catalogs, Gradle plugins, wrapper, Dockerfiles and Actions where present.
[codex] 2. Add official Gradle dependency submission on trusted default-branch pushes and
[codex]    a weekly/manual refresh. Use `cache-provider: basic`; submit with the job's
[codex]    GitHub token and the narrowly required `contents: write` permission.
[codex] 3. Configure coverage of all relevant modules/configurations, including runtime
[codex]    and test trees. Generate local evidence where credential-free resolution works;
[codex]    otherwise record graph verification as pending Phase 12. The acceptance proof
[codex]    must show Framework, Security, Tomcat, Netty and Jackson where actually resolved,
[codex]    not just the imported Boot BOM coordinate.
[codex] 4. Document inherited-dependency limits, visible major migrations and bot PR checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] Use the existing Node 24-ready Actions baseline. Keep submission on trusted code;
[codex] do not publish packages or enable Build Scan services to obtain a dependency graph.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Validate Renovate config, inspect extraction/lookup, and run actionlint. Generate
[codex] the official dependency snapshot locally where supported without credentials or
[codex] submission; check module and transitive coverage. Record authentication-dependent
[codex] generation/build checks as pending Phase 12. Hosted resolution and submission are
[codex] verified only in Phase 12.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] The graph-generation workflow and bot config pass local configuration checks.
[codex] Local transitive evidence is recorded where available; authenticated checks have
[codex] explicit Phase 12 handoffs. Selected versions are unchanged.

[codex] 
[codex] 1. Add the preset consumer and verify native manager extraction and update lookup.
[codex] 2. Add trusted-branch and weekly/manual graph submission with basic caching and
[codex]    existing package-secret references kept distinct from the submission token.
[codex] 3. Document the Phase 12 proof for resolved WebFlux/Reactor Netty/Netty and Spring
[codex]    dependencies, available local evidence, and the inherited-BOM limit.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] Do not add a Tomcat expectation to a service that does not resolve it. Prove
[codex] coverage from the actual graph rather than copying another service's package list.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Run config validation/extraction, credential-free lookup and actionlint. Generate
[codex] a local graph only where possible without credentials and inspect available Netty
[codex] evidence without overrides. Record authenticated build/resolution and complete
[codex] reactive graph verification as pending Phase 12.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] Local bot/workflow configuration checks and extraction pass, with available local
[codex] evidence and explicit Phase 12 handoffs. A complete remotely resolved reactive
[codex] runtime graph and credentials are not required to complete preparation.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Phase 8: Onboard the frontend and audit its lockfile
[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 
[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] for the whole Budget Analyzer stack. Bump and release it when
[codex] `spring-platform`, `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, or `service-web`
[codex] changes. Do not change it only because a service image, frontend artifact,
[codex] route, production overlay, `/api-docs` metadata file, or OCI deployment
[codex] configuration changed.
[codex] 
[codex] Java services can release runtime artifacts while continuing to consume the
[codex] same checked-in `serviceCommon` coordinate from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`.
[codex] The production deployment identity is the digest-pinned image inventory and
[codex] deployment manifest, which may contain mixed service artifact versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local Contributor Flow
[codex] 
[codex] The supported side-by-side workspace flow must stay credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] If you follow [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and run local
[codex] development through orchestration and Tilt:
[codex] 
[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 
[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 
[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 
[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex]   Local
[codex] 
[codex] If you hit a local `service-common` resolution error while working in the full
[codex] workspace, republish it locally and rerun the service build:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../service-common
[codex] ./gradlew clean build publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When GitHub Packages Credentials Are Required
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Packages credentials are a CI/release concern, not a getting-started
[codex] prerequisite.
[codex] 
[codex] You need GitHub Packages credentials when all of these are true:
[codex] 
[codex] - the build is not using the local orchestration/Tilt path
[codex] - `service-common` is not already available through Maven Local
[codex] - the build must resolve the published `org.budgetanalyzer` artifacts remotely
[codex]   from `https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common`
[codex] 
[codex] Typical examples:
[codex] 
[codex] - default GitHub Actions `build.yml` runs in `transaction-service`,
[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 
[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 
[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex]   Packages
[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 
[codex] ## GitHub Actions Environment
[codex] 
[codex] When a workflow intentionally resolves `service-common` remotely from GitHub
[codex] Packages, Gradle needs both a token and a package username.
[codex] 
[codex] For cross-repo private Maven/Gradle consumption, do not assume the workflow
[codex] repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can read `service-common`. GitHub still treats these
[codex] packages as repository-scoped, so the consuming workflow needs an explicit
[codex] GitHub Packages credential.
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the current repo wiring expects:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `GITHUB_ACTOR` here means the username that owns the GitHub Packages credential,
[codex] not necessarily `${{ github.actor }}`. `GITHUB_TOKEN` here means the secret used
[codex] for remote package reads, not necessarily the workflow repo's default token.
[codex] This requirement applies to the remote GitHub Packages path used by default
[codex] `build.yml` and release workflows. It does not apply to the normal `tilt up`
[codex] contributor flow.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Containerized Release Builds
[codex] 
[codex] The Java service Dockerfiles now support remote `service-common` resolution for
[codex] release and isolated CI builds without copying host `.m2` state into the build
[codex] context and without checking out sibling source trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Pass the workflow credentials into BuildKit as secrets:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] docker build \
[codex]   --secret id=github_actor,env=GITHUB_ACTOR \
[codex]   --secret id=github_token,env=GITHUB_TOKEN \
[codex]   -f Dockerfile .
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the release job should still expose those same credential
[codex] values under the env names the current Gradle builds read:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The Dockerfiles read those values only inside the Gradle builder-stage `RUN`
[codex] steps, so the credentials are not written into the image, filesystem layers, or
[codex] checked-in repo files.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Why Public Package Visibility Does Not Remove This
[codex] 
[codex] Making the `service-common` Maven packages public in the GitHub UI does not
[codex] remove the Maven/Gradle authentication requirement.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep the release workflow package-visibility and workflow-access cleanup, but do not
[codex] treat it as the fix for local contributor UX. The fix for local contributor UX
[codex] is keeping the side-by-side workspace and Tilt flow local-first and
[codex] credential-free.

[codex] # Session Gateway
[codex] 
[codex] > "Archetype: service. Role: Session-based edge authorization; manages OAuth2 flows, session cookies, and IDP grant validation."
[codex] >
[codex] > — [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#tree-position)
[codex] 
[codex] [![Build](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/session-gateway/actions/workflows/build.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/session-gateway/actions/workflows/build.yml)
[codex] 
[codex] Session-based edge authorization service that manages OAuth2 authentication flows for browser clients and writes session data as Redis hashes that the ext_authz HTTP service reads directly for per-request authorization at the Istio ingress.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Architecture
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] Browser → Istio Ingress (:443)
[codex]   ├─ /oauth2/*, /auth/*, /login/oauth2/*, /logout
[codex]   │      → Session Gateway (:8081) ← OAuth2 → Auth0
[codex]   │           ├─ Permission Service (:8086) [email/displayName]
[codex]   │           └─ Redis (:6379) [session:*]
[codex]   ├─ /login, /* → NGINX (:8080) → budget-analyzer-web
[codex]   └─ /api/* → ext-authz HTTP service (:9002) → NGINX (:8080) → Backend Services
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## What It Does
[codex] 
[codex] - Manages OAuth2 authorization-code flows with Auth0 for browser clients
[codex] - Fetches user roles and permissions from permission-service on login
[codex] - Writes session data (userId, roles, permissions, expiry) as Redis hashes (`session:{id}`)
[codex] - Issues HttpOnly session cookies; extends TTL via heartbeat (`GET /auth/v1/session`)
[codex] - Exposes internal session revocation for permission-service (`DELETE /internal/v1/sessions/users/{userId}`)
[codex] - ext_authz reads the same Redis hashes for ingress authorization — no separate schema
[codex] 
[codex] Bare `/login` is a frontend route served through NGINX. The real OAuth2 flow starts at `/oauth2/authorization/idp`.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Tech Stack
[codex] 
[codex] Spring WebFlux, Spring Security OAuth2 Client, custom Redis sessions via
[codex] `SessionWriter`/`SessionReader`, [service-common](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common)
[codex] (`spring-platform` dependency management plus reactive HTTP logging, correlation IDs, exception
[codex] handling from `service-web`).
[codex] 
[codex] ## Quick Start
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew build          # build
[codex] ./gradlew test           # test
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport  # coverage reports
[codex] ./gradlew bootRun        # run (requires Redis + permission-service)
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] JaCoCo coverage reports are written to
[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`. `check` enforces the
[codex] configured coverage gates.
[codex] 
[codex] Full local setup: [docs/local-development.md](docs/local-development.md)
[codex]

[codex] ## Phase 3: Onboard service-common and its resolved Java graph
[codex] 
[codex] ### Workspace
[codex] 
[codex] ../service-common
[codex] 
[codex] ### Goal
[codex] 
[codex] Discover platform/library updates and expose the actual BOM-managed dependency tree.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Scope
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate config, dependency-submission CI, and nearest repository documentation.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Non-goals
[codex] 
[codex] No BOM changes, overrides, library releases, build-logic redesign, or sibling edits.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Required context
[codex] 
[codex] Read this repository's `AGENTS.md`, version catalog, modules and build workflows,
[codex] plus orchestration's shared preset and service-common artifact-resolution guide.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Execution steps
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Add a thin Renovate config extending the shared preset. Verify extraction from
[codex]    catalogs, Gradle plugins, wrapper, Dockerfiles and Actions where present.
[codex] 2. Add official Gradle dependency submission on trusted default-branch pushes and
[codex]    a weekly/manual refresh. Use `cache-provider: basic`; submit with the job's
[codex]    GitHub token and the narrowly required `contents: write` permission.
[codex] 3. Configure coverage of all relevant modules/configurations, including runtime
[codex]    and test trees. Generate local evidence where credential-free resolution works;
[codex]    otherwise record graph verification as pending Phase 12. The acceptance proof
[codex]    must show Framework, Security, Tomcat, Netty and Jackson where actually resolved,
[codex]    not just the imported Boot BOM coordinate.
[codex] 4. Document inherited-dependency limits, visible major migrations and bot PR checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] Use the existing Node 24-ready Actions baseline. Keep submission on trusted code;
[codex] do not publish packages or enable Build Scan services to obtain a dependency graph.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Validate Renovate config, inspect extraction/lookup, and run actionlint. Generate
[codex] the official dependency snapshot locally where supported without credentials or
[codex] submission; check module and transitive coverage. Record authentication-dependent
[codex] generation/build checks as pending Phase 12. Hosted resolution and submission are
[codex] verified only in Phase 12.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] The graph-generation workflow and bot config pass local configuration checks.
[codex] Local transitive evidence is recorded where available; authenticated checks have
[codex] explicit Phase 12 handoffs. Selected versions are unchanged.
[codex]

[codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] 
[codex] The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] failure triage are owned by
[codex] [orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] This document records only the `service-common` integration and review checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Update discovery
[codex] 
[codex] `renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, and GitHub Actions managers discover this repository's
[codex] version catalog, build scripts, wrapper distribution, and workflow actions.
[codex] There are currently no Dockerfiles in this repository. Add file-specific
[codex] manager configuration only if a future dependency declaration is not covered
[codex] by a native manager.
[codex] 
[codex] The Phase 3 local extraction found 52 dependency occurrences across 12 files:
[codex] 33 Gradle occurrences in seven files, one wrapper occurrence, and 18 GitHub
[codex] Actions occurrences in four workflows. Maven and Gradle lookups produced 41
[codex] candidate branches before scheduling, concurrency limits, and dashboard
[codex] approval. Maintained-line and later-major proposals were both visible for the
[codex] Spring platform, documentation, test, locking, and wrapper dependencies. The
[codex] Actions references were extracted, but authenticated GitHub lookups remain a
[codex] post-publication hosted check because no GitHub token is supplied to local
[codex] validation.
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. It does not turn every
[codex] version inherited from the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, or Spring Modulith BOMs
[codex] into an independently updateable declaration. The resolved dependency graph
[codex] described below is the security inventory for those inherited versions.
[codex] 
[codex] Major updates remain visible in the Dependency Dashboard and require approval.
[codex] In particular, Spring Boot 4 and the related Spring Cloud, Spring Framework,
[codex] Spring Security, Spring Modulith, SpringDoc, Testcontainers, ShedLock, and test
[codex] platform lines are a compatibility migration, not a routine patch. Renovate
[codex] cannot select the correct release-train combination or coordinate consumer
[codex] services by itself.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] `basic` cache provider. Its job token receives only `contents: write`, which is
[codex] required by GitHub's Dependency Submission API. The graph is submitted
[codex] directly and is not retained as a workflow artifact or published as a Build
[codex] Scan.
[codex] 
[codex] The action's default resolution task visits all projects and all resolvable
[codex] configurations. Do not add project or configuration filters without proving
[codex] equivalent coverage: the platform modules contribute imported BOMs, while the
[codex] library modules contribute compile, runtime, and test dependency trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Local Phase 3 validation generated an official plugin snapshot without
[codex] submitting it. The snapshot contained 216 resolved coordinate/version entries
[codex] across the four subprojects. It included the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and
[codex] Spring Modulith BOM coordinates and these inherited component families where
[codex] the current build actually resolves them:
[codex] 
[codex] | Component family | Resolution evidence |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Framework | Library runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] | Spring Security | `service-core` and `service-web` test classpaths; compile-only APIs are also resolved through compile classpaths |
[codex] | Embedded Tomcat | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the servlet stack |
[codex] | Netty | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the reactive stack |
[codex] | Jackson | `service-core` and `service-web` runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] 
[codex] The entry count and resolved versions will change as the checked-in dependency
[codex] selection changes; they are evidence from onboarding, not a desired-version
[codex] inventory. A successful local snapshot proves generation and transitive
[codex] coverage, but only a hosted run after repository activation proves submission
[codex] and creates Dependabot alert input.
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub's dependency graph reports resolved packages but does not decide which
[codex] BOM or override should remediate an inherited finding. A Dependabot alert is
[codex] also not proof that an available Spring Boot update fixes every Framework,
[codex] Security, Jackson, Tomcat, or Netty advisory. Trace the affected configuration
[codex] with Gradle dependency insight and review any override as a service-owner
[codex] decision.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Bot pull request checks
[codex] 
[codex] For every Renovate pull request:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Keep the shared preset's no-automerge and dashboard-approval behavior.
[codex] 2. Review the resolved dependency diff, release notes, Java 25 and Spring
[codex]    release-train compatibility, and whether an inherited advisory is actually
[codex]    remediated.
[codex] 3. Run the repository-required validation in order:
[codex] 
[codex]    ```bash
[codex]    ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex]    ./gradlew clean build
[codex]    ```
[codex] 
[codex] 4. For platform or shared-library changes, validate all consuming services
[codex]    before release as required by
[codex]    [versioning and compatibility](versioning-and-compatibility.md). Do not
[codex]    publish artifacts or update consumers from an unreviewed bot branch.
[codex] 
[codex] Graph generation failures are failures, not clean security results. Preserve
[codex] dependency-resolution and submission errors for triage; do not substitute
[codex] Maven Local, omit configurations, or enable a hosted Build Scan to make the job
[codex] pass.

[codex] 1330-[codex] 
[codex] 1331:[codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] 1332-[codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] 1333-[codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] 1334-[codex] `basic` cache provider. It resolves all projects and all resolvable
[codex] 1335-[codex] configurations so application, runtime, build, and test dependency trees are
[codex] 1336-[codex] included. Do not add configuration filters without proving equivalent coverage.
[codex] 1337-[codex] 
[codex] 1338-[codex] Remote resolution uses `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` and
[codex] 1339-[codex] `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, exposed to Gradle as `GITHUB_ACTOR` and

[codex] 1576-[codex] 1856:[codex] [codex] # The `dependency-submission` action
[codex] 1577-[codex] 1858:[codex] [codex] The `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action provides the simplest (and recommended) way to generate a 
[codex] 1578-[codex] 1862:[codex] [codex] The dependency graph snapshot is generated via integration with the [GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin), and submitted to your repository via the 
[codex] 1579-[codex] 1863:[codex] [codex] [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] 1580-[codex] 1864:[codex] [codex] The generated snapshot files can be submitted in the same job, or saved for submission in a subsequent job.
[codex] 1581-[codex] 1871:[codex] [codex] If you're confused by the behaviour you're seeing or have specific questions, please check out [the FAQ](dependency-submission-faq.md) before raising an issue.
[codex] 1582:[codex] 1887:[codex] [codex] Simply add this as a new workflow file to your repository (eg `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml`).
[codex] 1583-[codex] 1900:[codex] [codex]   dependency-submission:
[codex] 1584-[codex] 1910:[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 1585-[codex] 1915:[codex] [codex] To generate a dependency graph, the `dependency-submission` action must perform a Gradle execution that resolves
[codex] 1586-[codex] 1940:[codex] [codex] You can automatically publish a free Develocity Build Scan on every run of `gradle/actions/dependency-submission`. 
[codex] 1587-[codex] 1946:[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 1588-[codex] 1961:[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 1589-[codex] 1992:[codex] [codex] See the [Action Metadata file](../dependency-submission/action.yml) for a more detailed description of each input parameter.
[codex] 1590-[codex] 1996:[codex] [codex] These will be automatically set by the `dependency-submission` action, but you may override these values

[codex] 2489-[codex] directly and is not retained as a workflow artifact or published as a Build

[codex] 2616-[codex] [codex] +## Update discovery
[codex] 2617-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2618:[codex] [codex] +`renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] 2619-[codex] [codex] +Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, Dockerfile, and GitHub Actions managers discover this
[codex] 2620-[codex] [codex] +repository's version catalog, build script, wrapper distribution, base images,
[codex] 2621-[codex] [codex] +and workflow actions. The Gradle catalog extraction includes the declared
[codex] 2622-[codex] [codex] +`serviceCommon` version used by `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, and
[codex] 2623-[codex] [codex] +`service-web`.
[codex] 2624-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2625-[codex] [codex] +`service-common` is hosted in GitHub Packages. Extraction does not prove that
[codex] 2626-[codex] [codex] +Renovate can look up that private Maven coordinate. Before activation, configure
[codex] --
[codex] 2632-[codex] [codex] +Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. Versions inherited from
[codex] 2633-[codex] [codex] +the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and Spring Modulith BOMs remain represented by
[codex] 2634-[codex] [codex] +the resolved dependency graph described below. A direct Spring Boot or
[codex] 2635-[codex] [codex] +`serviceCommon` proposal is not proof that every inherited vulnerability is
[codex] 2636-[codex] [codex] +fixed.
[codex] 2637-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2638-[codex] [codex] +## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 2639-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2640:[codex] [codex] +`.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] 2641-[codex] [codex] +weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] 2642-[codex] [codex] +the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] 2643-[codex] [codex] +`basic` cache provider. It resolves all projects and all resolvable
[codex] 2644-[codex] [codex] +configurations so application, runtime, build, and test dependency trees are
[codex] 2645-[codex] [codex] +included. Do not add configuration filters without proving equivalent coverage.
[codex] 2646-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2647-[codex] [codex] +Remote resolution uses `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` and
[codex] 2648-[codex] [codex] +`SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, exposed to Gradle as `GITHUB_ACTOR` and
[codex] --
[codex] 2674-[codex] [codex] +```
[codex] 2675-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2676-[codex] [codex] +Bot pull requests use the existing `build.yml` pull-request workflow. Forked or
[codex] 2677-[codex] [codex] +otherwise untrusted pull requests do not receive package-read secrets, so a
[codex] 2678-[codex] [codex] +failure to resolve `service-common` there is an unavailable-secret condition,
[codex] 2679-[codex] [codex] +not evidence that the dependency is absent. Do not add
[codex] 2680-[codex] [codex] +`pull_request_target` or expose package credentials to untrusted dependency
[codex] 2681-[codex] [codex] +branches to bypass that boundary.

[codex] 2930-[codex] [codex] +# Dependency Automation
[codex] 2931-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2932-[codex] [codex] +The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] 2933-[codex] [codex] +failure triage are owned by
[codex] 2934:[codex] [codex] +[orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] 2935-[codex] [codex] +This document records only the `currency-service` integration and review checks.
[codex] 2936-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2937-[codex] [codex] +## Update discovery
[codex] 2938-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2939:[codex] [codex] +`renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] 2940-[codex] [codex] +Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, Dockerfile, and GitHub Actions managers discover this
[codex] 2941-[codex] [codex] +repository's version catalog, build script, wrapper distribution, base images,
[codex] 2942-[codex] [codex] +and workflow actions. The Gradle catalog extraction includes the declared
[codex] 2943-[codex] [codex] +`serviceCommon` version used by `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, and
[codex] 2944-[codex] [codex] +`service-web`.
[codex] 2945-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2946-[codex] [codex] +`service-common` is hosted in GitHub Packages. Extraction does not prove that
[codex] 2947-[codex] [codex] +Renovate can look up that private Maven coordinate. Before activation, configure
[codex] --
[codex] 2953-[codex] [codex] +Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. Versions inherited from
[codex] 2954-[codex] [codex] +the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and Spring Modulith BOMs remain represented by
[codex] 2955-[codex] [codex] +the resolved dependency graph described below. A direct Spring Boot or
[codex] 2956-[codex] [codex] +`serviceCommon` proposal is not proof that every inherited vulnerability is
[codex] 2957-[codex] [codex] +fixed.
[codex] 2958-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2959-[codex] [codex] +## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 2960-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2961:[codex] [codex] +`.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] 2962-[codex] [codex] +weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] 2963-[codex] [codex] +the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] 2964-[codex] [codex] +`basic` cache provider. It resolves all projects and all resolvable
[codex] 2965-[codex] [codex] +configurations so application, runtime, build, and test dependency trees are
[codex] 2966-[codex] [codex] +included. Do not add configuration filters without proving equivalent coverage.
[codex] 2967-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2968-[codex] [codex] +Remote resolution uses `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` and
[codex] 2969-[codex] [codex] +`SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, exposed to Gradle as `GITHUB_ACTOR` and
[codex] --
[codex] 2995-[codex] [codex] +```
[codex] 2996-[codex] [codex] +
[codex] 2997-[codex] [codex] +Bot pull requests use the existing `build.yml` pull-request workflow. Forked or
[codex] 2998-[codex] [codex] +otherwise untrusted pull requests do not receive package-read secrets, so a
[codex] 2999-[codex] [codex] +failure to resolve `service-common` there is an unavailable-secret condition,
[codex] 3000-[codex] [codex] +not evidence that the dependency is absent. Do not add
[codex] 3001-[codex] [codex] +`pull_request_target` or expose package credentials to untrusted dependency
[codex] 3002-[codex] [codex] +branches to bypass that boundary.

[codex] 3900-[codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] 3901-[codex] 
[codex] 3902-[codex] The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] 3903-[codex] failure triage are owned by
[codex] 3904:[codex] [orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] 3905-[codex] This document records only the `currency-service` integration and review checks.
[codex] 3906-[codex] 
[codex] 3907-[codex] ## Update discovery
[codex] 3908-[codex] 
[codex] 3909:[codex] `renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] 3910-[codex] Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, Dockerfile, and GitHub Actions managers discover this
[codex] 3911-[codex] repository's version catalog, build script, wrapper distribution, base images,
[codex] 3912-[codex] and workflow actions. The Gradle catalog extraction includes the declared
[codex] 3913-[codex] `serviceCommon` version used by `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, and
[codex] 3914-[codex] `service-web`.
[codex] 3915-[codex] 
[codex] 3916-[codex] `service-common` is hosted in GitHub Packages. Extraction does not prove that
[codex] 3917-[codex] Renovate can look up that private Maven coordinate. Before activation, configure
[codex] --
[codex] 3931-[codex] Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. Versions inherited from
[codex] 3932-[codex] the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and Spring Modulith BOMs remain represented by
[codex] 3933-[codex] the resolved dependency graph described below. A direct Spring Boot or
[codex] 3934-[codex] `serviceCommon` proposal is not proof that every inherited vulnerability is
[codex] 3935-[codex] fixed.
[codex] 3936-[codex] 
[codex] 3937-[codex] ## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 3938-[codex] 
[codex] 3939:[codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] 3940-[codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] 3941-[codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] 3942-[codex] `basic` cache provider. It resolves all projects and all resolvable
[codex] 3943-[codex] configurations so application, runtime, build, and test dependency trees are
[codex] 3944-[codex] included. Do not add configuration filters without proving equivalent coverage.
[codex] 3945-[codex] 
[codex] 3946-[codex] Remote resolution uses `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` and
[codex] 3947-[codex] `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, exposed to Gradle as `GITHUB_ACTOR` and
[codex] --
[codex] 3974-[codex] 
[codex] 3975-[codex] Bot pull requests use the existing `build.yml` pull-request workflow. Forked or
[codex] 3976-[codex] otherwise untrusted pull requests do not receive package-read secrets, so a
[codex] 3977-[codex] failure to resolve `service-common` there is an unavailable-secret condition,
[codex] 3978-[codex] not evidence that the dependency is absent. Do not add
[codex] 3979-[codex] `pull_request_target` or expose package credentials to untrusted dependency
[codex] 3980-[codex] branches to bypass that boundary.
[codex] 3981-[codex]

[codex] [codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] [codex] failure triage are owned by
[codex] [codex] [orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] [codex] This document records only the `currency-service` integration and review checks.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Update discovery
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] `renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] [codex] Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, Dockerfile, and GitHub Actions managers discover this
[codex] [codex] repository's version catalog, build script, wrapper distribution, base images,
[codex] [codex] and workflow actions. The Gradle catalog extraction includes the declared
[codex] [codex] `serviceCommon` version used by `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, and
[codex] [codex] `service-web`.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] `service-common` is hosted in GitHub Packages. Extraction does not prove that
[codex] [codex] Renovate can look up that private Maven coordinate. Before activation, configure
[codex] [codex] the Mend Renovate Community App's supported encrypted Maven credentials and
[codex] [codex] verify an authenticated lookup; never put a package token in this repository or
[codex] [codex] the shared preset. The app must keep direct, maintained-line, and later-major
[codex] [codex] proposals visible where each datasource supports them.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The Phase 4 local extraction found 57 dependency occurrences across eight files:
[codex] [codex] 37 Gradle occurrences in three files, one wrapper occurrence, three Dockerfile
[codex] [codex] occurrences, and 16 GitHub Actions occurrences in three workflows. Public
[codex] [codex] Gradle, wrapper, and image lookups produced candidate updates. GitHub Actions
[codex] [codex] lookups and the three `org.budgetanalyzer` Maven coordinates remain
[codex] [codex] authenticated hosted checks; the local runner had no GitHub or package-read
[codex] [codex] token, so those checks are pending rather than passed.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. Versions inherited from
[codex] [codex] the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and Spring Modulith BOMs remain represented by
[codex] [codex] the resolved dependency graph described below. A direct Spring Boot or
[codex] [codex] `serviceCommon` proposal is not proof that every inherited vulnerability is
[codex] [codex] fixed.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] [codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] [codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] [codex] `basic` cache provider. It resolves all projects and all resolvable
[codex] [codex] configurations so application, runtime, build, and test dependency trees are
[codex] [codex] included. Do not add configuration filters without proving equivalent coverage.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Remote resolution uses `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` and
[codex] [codex] `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, exposed to Gradle as `GITHUB_ACTOR` and
[codex] [codex] `GITHUB_TOKEN`. These package-read credentials are distinct from
[codex] [codex] `${{ github.token }}`, which the action uses to submit the graph with the job's
[codex] [codex] only elevated permission, `contents: write`. The snapshot is submitted directly
[codex] [codex] and is not retained as an artifact or published as a Build Scan.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The workflow must fail when package credentials are missing, the pinned
[codex] [codex] `service-common` artifacts cannot be resolved, graph generation is incomplete,
[codex] [codex] or GitHub rejects submission. Do not substitute Maven Local, omit the internal
[codex] [codex] dependency, filter failed configurations, or treat graph-generation failure as
[codex] [codex] a clean security result. A hosted run on `main` is the final proof that GitHub
[codex] [codex] accepted the submission and can produce Dependabot alerts from it.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Bot pull request checks
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Every Renovate pull request remains non-automerged. Review the resolved
[codex] [codex] dependency diff, release notes, Java 25 and Spring release-train compatibility,
[codex] [codex] and whether the proposed direct dependency actually remediates any inherited
[codex] [codex] alert. Major updates remain visible in the Dependency Dashboard and require
[codex] [codex] approval.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Run the required repository validation in order:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Bot pull requests use the existing `build.yml` pull-request workflow. Forked or
[codex] [codex] otherwise untrusted pull requests do not receive package-read secrets, so a
[codex] [codex] failure to resolve `service-common` there is an unavailable-secret condition,
[codex] [codex] not evidence that the dependency is absent. Do not add
[codex] [codex] `pull_request_target` or expose package credentials to untrusted dependency
[codex] [codex] branches to bypass that boundary.
[codex] [codex] # Currency Service
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] > "Archetype: service. Role: Manages currencies and exchange rates with external provider integration."
[codex] [codex] >
[codex] [codex] > — [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#tree-position)
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] [![Build](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml)
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Spring Boot microservice that automates exchange rate imports from the [Federal Reserve Economic Data (FRED)](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/) API, delivering reliable currency and exchange rate data for Budget Analyzer.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Core Capabilities
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - **Automated FRED imports** - Daily scheduled imports with provider abstraction ([details](docs/fred-integration.md))
[codex] [codex] - **Currency series management** - CRUD operations for exchange rate time series ([API docs](docs/api/README.md))
[codex] [codex] - **Historical rate queries** - Date range filtering with Redis caching (80-95% hit rate)
[codex] [codex] - **Distributed coordination** - Multi-pod safe scheduling with ShedLock
[codex] [codex] - **Event-driven architecture** - Guaranteed delivery via transactional outbox + RabbitMQ
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Quick Start
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Prerequisites:** JDK 25, Docker/Docker Compose
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] # 1. Start infrastructure
[codex] [codex] cd ../orchestration && tilt up
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # 2. Export credentials and start the service
[codex] [codex] cd ../currency-service
[codex] [codex] export FRED_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
[codex] [codex] export SPRING_DATASOURCE_PASSWORD=your_db_password
[codex] [codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PASSWORD=your_rabbitmq_password
[codex] [codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PASSWORD=your_redis_password
[codex] [codex] # See docs/local-development.md for full env var list (TLS, ports, etc.)
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew bootRun
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Dev server:** `http://localhost:8084` | **Swagger UI:** `http://localhost:8084/swagger-ui.html`
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Java 25, Spring Boot 3.x (Web, Data JPA, Modulith, Actuator), PostgreSQL, Redis, RabbitMQ, ShedLock, Flyway, SpringDoc OpenAPI, TestContainers + JUnit 5
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Build and Test
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] JaCoCo coverage reports are written to
[codex] [codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] [codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`. `check` enforces the
[codex] [codex] configured coverage gates.

[codex] 2560-[codex] [codex] ### Environment variables
[codex] 2561-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2562-[codex] [codex] The following environment variables configure the snapshot generated by the `GitHubDependencyExtractorPlugin`. See the [GitHub Dependency Submission API docs](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission?apiVersion=2022-11-28) for details:
[codex] 2563-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2564-[codex] [codex] #### Required environment variables
[codex] 2565-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2566-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_CORRELATOR`: Sets the `job.correlator` value for the dependency submission
[codex] 2567-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_ID`: Sets the `job.id` value for the dependency submission
[codex] 2568-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REF`: Sets the `ref` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] 2569-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_SHA`: Sets the `sha` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] 2570-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_WORKSPACE`: Sets the root directory of the github repository. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] 2571:[codex] [codex] - `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` (optional): Specifies where the dependency graph report will be generated. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] 2572-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2573-[codex] [codex] #### Optional environment variables
[codex] 2574-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2575-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_NAME`: Sets the `detector.name` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin`
[codex] 2576-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_VERSION`: Sets the `detector.version` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to current version of the plugin.
[codex] 2577-[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_URL`: Sets the `detector.url` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin`
[codex] 2578-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2579-[codex] [codex] Each of these values can also be provided via a system property. 
[codex] 2580:[codex] [codex] eg: Env var `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` can be set with `-DDEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR=...` on the command-line.
[codex] 2581-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2582-[codex] [codex] ### Filtering which Gradle Configurations contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 2583-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2584-[codex] [codex] If you do not want to include every dependency configuration in every project in your build, you can limit the
[codex] 2585-[codex] [codex] dependency extraction to a subset of these.
[codex] 2586-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2587-[codex] [codex] The following parameters control the set of projects and configurations that contribute dependencies.
[codex] 2588-[codex] [codex] Each of these is a regular expression value, and can set either as an environment variable or as a system property on the command line.
[codex] 2589-[codex] [codex] 
[codex] 2590-[codex] [codex] | Property                                | Description               | Default                         |
[codex] 2591-[codex] [codex] |-----------------------------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|
[codex] 2592-[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_INCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects to include       | All projects are included       |
[codex] --

[codex] +  submit-gradle-dependencies:
[codex] +    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
[codex] +    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] +    permissions:
[codex] +      contents: write
[codex] +
[codex] +    steps:
[codex] +      - name: Checkout code
[codex] +        uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          persist-credentials: false
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Set up JDK 25
[codex] +        uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          java-version: '25'
[codex] +          distribution: 'temurin'
[codex] +

[codex] +        shell: bash

[codex] +          PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] +        run: |
[codex] +          set -euo pipefail
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ -z "${PACKAGES_USERNAME}" || -z "${PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN}" ]]; then
[codex] +            echo "Missing SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME or SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN." >&2
[codex] +            exit 1
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +

[codex] +          if [[ -z "${service_common_version}" ]]; then
[codex] +            echo "Could not read serviceCommon from gradle/libs.versions.toml." >&2
[codex] +            exit 1
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +          for artifact in spring-platform service-web; do
[codex] +            pom_url="https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common/org/budgetanalyzer/${artifact}/${service_common_version}/${artifact}-${service_common_version}.pom"
[codex] +            curl --fail --silent --show-error \
[codex] +              --user "${PACKAGES_USERNAME}:${PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN}" \
[codex] +              --output /dev/null \
[codex] +              "${pom_url}"
[codex] +          done

[codex] +        uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
